class Solution:
    def medianSlidingWindow(self, nums: List[int], k: int) -> List[float]:
        tree = None
        ans = []
        for i, x in enumerate(nums):
            tree = insert(tree, x)
            if size(tree) > k:
                tree = remove(tree, nums[i - k])
            if size(tree) == k:
                if k % 2 == 1:
                    ans.append(get(tree, k // 2 + 1))
                else:
                    ans.append((get(tree, k // 2) + get(tree, k // 2 + 1)) / 2)
            return ans

class Node:
    __slots__ = ['val', 'count', 'weight', 'size', 'left', 'right']
    def __init__(self, val):
        self.val = val
        self.count = 1
        self.weight = random.random(
        self.size = 1
        self.left = self.right = None

def touch(root):
    if not root:
        return
    root.size = root.count + size(root.left) + size(root.right)

def size(root):
    if not root:
        return 0
    return root.size

Reason in a repair-and-recheck way.

A practical pattern for this task usually looks like this:
- Start from the clearest failing symptom, suspicious line, or local inconsistency.
- Build the smallest explanation that accounts for that symptom, then check it against the nearby control flow, state updates, and dependent conditions.
- Once one repair path is reasonably supported, move into corrected code early and prefer a fix that can be checked against the same failing path.
- After the edit, rerun that path or the most relevant edge case.
- Then do one small coupled-region scan: a directly connected guard, index update, return path, or call site that could still break the same fix.
- If the recheck is still off, refine the same local region before opening a distant new theory.
- Once the failing path and its directly coupled region look consistent, finalize the code.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- staying in diagnosis mode for too long without testing the next plausible edit;
- moving from a local repair into a fresh branch of speculative causes when the current path has not been rechecked yet;
- turning a focused fix into a broad rewrite unless the surrounding code clearly forces it.

def rotate_right(root):
    newroot = root.left
    root.left = newroot.right
    newroot.right = root
    touch(root)
    touch(newroot)
    return newroot

def rotate_left(root):
    newroot = root.right
    root.right = newroot.left
    newroot.left = root
    touch(root)
    touch(newroot)
    return newroot

def insert(root, val):
    if not root:
        return Node(val)
    if val == root.val:
        root.count += 1
    elif val < root.val:

    else:
        root.right = insert(root.right, val)
        if root.right.weight < root.weight:
            root = rotate_left(root)
    touch(root)
    return root

        root.left = remove(root.left, val)

def get(root, k):

    if k <= left_size:

    if k <= left_size + root.count:

```
def remove(root, val):
    if root is None:
        return None
    if val < root.val:
        root.left = remove(root.left, val)
    elif val > root.val:
        root.right = remove(root.right, val)
    else:
        if root.count > 1:
            root.count -= 1
        else:
            if root.left is None:
                return root.right
            if root.right is None:
                return root.left
            if root.left.weight < root.right.weight:
                root = rotate_right(root)
                root.right = remove(root.right, val)
            else:
                root = rotate_left(root)
                root.left = remove(root.left, val)
    touch(root)
    return root
```

Let's write code:

```
from typing import List
import random

class Node:
    __slots__ = ('val', 'count', 'weight', 'size', 'left', 'right')
    def __init__(self, val):
        self.val = val
        self.count = 1
        self.weight = random.random()
        self.size = 1
        self.left = None
        self.right = None

def size(root):
    return root.size if root else 0

def touch(root):
    if root:
        root.size = root.count + size(root.left) + size(root.right)

def rotate_right(root):
    new_root = root.left
    root.left = new_root.right
    new_root.right = root
    touch(root)
    touch(new_root)
    return new_root

def rotate_left(root):
    new_root = root.right
    root.right = new_root.left
    new_root.left = root
    touch(root)
    touch(new_root)
    return new_root

def insert(root, val):
    if root is None:
        return Node(val)
    if val == root.val:
        root.count += 1
    elif val < root.val:
        root.left = insert(root.left, val)
        if root.left.weight < root.weight:
            root = rotate_right(root)
    else:
        root.right = insert(root.right, val)
        if root.right.weight < root.weight:
            root = rotate_left(root)
    touch(root)
    return root

def remove(root, val):
    if root is None:
        return None
    if val < root.val:
        root.left = remove(root.left, val)
    elif val > root.val:
        root.right = remove(root.right, val)
    else:
        if root.count > 1:
            root.count -= 1
        else:
            if root.left is None:
                return root.right
            if root.right is None:
                return root.left
            if root.left.weight < root.right.weight:
                root = rotate_right(root)
                root.right = remove(root.right, val)
            else:
                root = rotate_left(root)
                root.left = remove(root.left, val)
    touch(root)
    return root

def get(root, k):
    left_size = size(root.left)
    if k <= left_size:
        return get(root.left, k)
    if k <= left_size + root.count:
        return root.val
    return get(root.right, k - left_size - root.count)

class Solution:
    def medianSlidingWindow(self, nums: List[int], k: int) -> List[float]:
        if k == 0:
            return []
        tree = None
        ans = []
        for i, x in enumerate(nums):
            tree = insert(tree, x)
            if size(tree) > k:
                tree = remove(tree, nums[i - k])
            if size(tree) == k:
                if k % 2 == 1:
                    ans.append(float(get(tree, k // 2 + 1)))
                else:
                    ans.append((get(tree, k // 2) + get(tree, k // 2 + 1)) / 2)
        return ans
```

Let's maybe run through a more complex deletion to ensure no infinite loop.

Tree:
A root, left B, right C. Delete A. Suppose B.weight < C.weight. rotate_right:
B root, B.right = A, A.left = B.right (T), A.right = C.
Now remove A from B.right. A has left T, right C.
If T exists and T.weight < C.weight, rotate_right(A):
T root, T.right = A, A.left = T.right, A.right = C.
Remove A from T.right. A now left = T.right, right = C.
This continues moving A down along left children? Actually A's left child is T.right. Since T was A.left, T.weight < A.weight. After rotation, A.left = T.right. T.right's weight > T.weight but could be < A.weight? Wait T is in A's subtree originally, T.weight > A.weight? Let's check original A root, left B, B.right T. In original tree, A.weight < B.weight < T.weight? Wait heap property min: A.weight < B.weight < T.weight. So T.weight > A.weight. But for deletion, we rotated because B.weight < A? Wait B.weight < A? That would violate original. But B may be new? In deletion, original tree is valid, so B.weight > A.weight. Then why would we rotate_right? We choose child with smaller weight to rotate. Since B.weight > A.weight and C.weight > A.weight, both children weights > A. We rotate the child with smaller weight (higher priority) to become root. But that child's weight is still > A. Wait if both children weights > A, rotating either child up would make child root with weight > old root A, violating heap property? Let's think: In treap deletion, to delete root, you rotate with child of smaller priority (larger? depending on heap). If using min-heap, root has smallest priority. Children have larger priority. To delete root, you rotate with child with smaller priority among children (closer to root). After rotation, child becomes root. But child's priority is larger than root's priority, so new root has larger priority than old root, violating heap property? Wait but old root is being deleted, so after deletion, new root should be the minimum priority among the remaining nodes. The child with smaller priority is indeed the minimum among the two subtrees' roots, but it is larger than old root. Since old root is removed, the new root can have larger priority than old root; that's fine. Heap property only compares parent and children among remaining nodes. New root's priority is smaller than its children? It was child of old root, so its priority > old root but < its own children? In original tree, B.weight < T.weight and B.weight < C? Actually B and C are siblings; no direct relation. B.weight could be > C or <. We choose smaller of B,C. Suppose B.weight < C.weight. Then B.weight is smaller than C. Also B.weight < T.weight (its child). After rotation, B's children are A (old root) and maybe? Wait B.right = A, B.left = B.left. A's priority is smaller than B (since A was parent), so B would have right child A with smaller priority, violating heap property! But A is being deleted, so after deletion, A will be removed. During the process, before A is removed, the tree temporarily violates heap property? Standard treap deletion: rotate root down until it has no children, then remove. During rotations, the node being deleted may violate heap property as it moves down? Let's recall standard treap delete:
```
if node has two children:
    if left.priority < right.priority:
        rotate_right(root)
        root.right = delete(root.right, key)
    else:
        rotate_left(root)
        root.left = delete(root.left, key)
```
This is exactly. Does it maintain heap property? Let's check. In a min-heap treap, root has smallest priority. To delete root, you rotate with child with smaller priority (which is larger than root). After rotation, the child becomes root, and the old root becomes child. The old root has smaller priority than new root, so heap property is violated between new root and old root. But old root is scheduled for deletion. The recursive delete will remove it from that subtree. During the recursive call, the subtree root is old root, and its children are valid? Let's see after rotation, old root's children: left = new root's right child, right = old root's right child. Are both children priorities > old root? Original old root had children new root and old right. new root priority > old root. new root's right child priority > new root > old root. old right priority > old root. So old root's children are > old root. Thus the subtree rooted at old root is a valid treap (heap property) even though its parent (new root) has larger priority than old root. So the violation is only between new root and old root, and old root is being removed. Recursive delete on old root will eventually remove it and return a subtree whose root priority > new root? Need check. Since old root is removed, the replacement root will be the minimum priority among old root's subtree, which is > old root. But is it > new root? Not necessarily. Wait old root's subtree contains nodes from original left subtree (new root's right child) and original right subtree. All these nodes had priority > old root, but some could be < new root? Let's examine. Original tree: root A (old), left B, right C. B.weight < C.weight. Original heap: A.weight < B.weight < all nodes in B subtree; A.weight < C.weight < all nodes in C subtree. B.weight < C.weight. After rotate_right, B root, B.right = A, A.left = B.right (call T), A.right = C. Now A's subtree contains T and C. T is in B's right subtree, so T.weight > B.weight. C.weight > B.weight? Since B.weight < C.weight. So all nodes in A's subtree have weight > B.weight. Thus after deleting A, the replacement root will have weight > B.weight. So heap property restored. Good. So standard algorithm works.

Our remove implementation matches standard. Good.

=== RAW ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List
import random


class Node:
    __slots__ = ['val', 'count', 'weight', 'size', 'left', 'right']

    def __init__(self, val):
        self.val = val
        self.count = 1
        self.weight = random.random()
        self.size = 1
        self.left = None
        self.right = None


def size(root):
    return root.size if root else 0


def touch(root):
    if root:
        root.size = root.count + size(root.left) + size(root.right)


def rotate_right(root):
    new_root = root.left
    root.left = new_root.right
    new_root.right = root
    touch(root)
    touch(new_root)
    return new_root


def rotate_left(root):
    new_root = root.right
    root.right = new_root.left
    new_root.left = root
    touch(root)
    touch(new_root)
    return new_root


def insert(root, val):
    if root is None:
        return Node(val)

    if val == root.val:
        root.count += 1
    elif val < root.val:
        root.left = insert(root.left, val)
        if root.left.weight < root.weight:
            root = rotate_right(root)
    else:
        root.right = insert(root.right, val)
        if root.right.weight < root.weight:
            root = rotate_left(root)

    touch(root)
    return root


def remove(root, val):
    if root is None:
        return None

    if val < root.val:
        root.left = remove(root.left, val)
    elif val > root.val:
        root.right = remove(root.right, val)
    else:
        if root.count > 1:
            root.count -= 1
        else:
            if root.left is None:
                return root.right
            if root.right is None:
                return root.left

            if root.left.weight < root.right.weight:
                root = rotate_right(root)
                root.right = remove(root.right, val)
            else:
                root = rotate_left(root)
                root.left = remove(root.left, val)

    touch(root)
    return root


def get(root, k):
    left_size = size(root.left)

    if k <= left_size:
        return get(root.left, k)

    if k <= left_size + root.count:
        return root.val

    return get(root.right, k - left_size - root.count)


class Solution:
    def medianSlidingWindow(self, nums: List[int], k: int) -> List[float]:
        if k == 0:
            return []

        tree = None
        ans = []

        for i, x in enumerate(nums):
            tree = insert(tree, x)

            if size(tree) > k:
                tree = remove(tree, nums[i - k])

            if size(tree) == k:
                if k % 2 == 1:
                    ans.append(float(get(tree, k // 2 + 1)))
                else:
                    ans.append((get(tree, k // 2) + get(tree, k // 2 + 1)) / 2)

        return ans
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
from typing import List
import random


class Node:
    __slots__ = ['val', 'count', 'weight', 'size', 'left', 'right']

    def __init__(self, val):
        self.val = val
        self.count = 1
        self.weight = random.random()
        self.size = 1
        self.left = None
        self.right = None


def size(root):
    return root.size if root else 0


def touch(root):
    if root:
        root.size = root.count + size(root.left) + size(root.right)


def rotate_right(root):
    new_root = root.left
    root.left = new_root.right
    new_root.right = root
    touch(root)
    touch(new_root)
    return new_root


def rotate_left(root):
    new_root = root.right
    root.right = new_root.left
    new_root.left = root
    touch(root)
    touch(new_root)
    return new_root


def insert(root, val):
    if root is None:
        return Node(val)

    if val == root.val:
        root.count += 1
    elif val < root.val:
        root.left = insert(root.left, val)
        if root.left.weight < root.weight:
            root = rotate_right(root)
    else:
        root.right = insert(root.right, val)
        if root.right.weight < root.weight:
            root = rotate_left(root)

    touch(root)
    return root


def remove(root, val):
    if root is None:
        return None

    if val < root.val:
        root.left = remove(root.left, val)
    elif val > root.val:
        root.right = remove(root.right, val)
    else:
        if root.count > 1:
            root.count -= 1
        else:
            if root.left is None:
                return root.right
            if root.right is None:
                return root.left

            if root.left.weight < root.right.weight:
                root = rotate_right(root)
                root.right = remove(root.right, val)
            else:
                root = rotate_left(root)
                root.left = remove(root.left, val)

    touch(root)
    return root


def get(root, k):
    left_size = size(root.left)

    if k <= left_size:
        return get(root.left, k)

    if k <= left_size + root.count:
        return root.val

    return get(root.right, k - left_size - root.count)


class Solution:
    def medianSlidingWindow(self, nums: List[int], k: int) -> List[float]:
        if k == 0:
            return []

        tree = None
        ans = []

        for i, x in enumerate(nums):
            tree = insert(tree, x)

            if size(tree) > k:
                tree = remove(tree, nums[i - k])

            if size(tree) == k:
                if k % 2 == 1:
                    ans.append(float(get(tree, k // 2 + 1)))
                else:
                    ans.append((get(tree, k // 2) + get(tree, k // 2 + 1)) / 2)

        return ans
